e(z,v,\mu+\delta_{(y,u)})=e(z,v,\mu+\delta_{(y,u)}+\delta_{(x,s)})\quad\text{and}\quad e(z,v,\mu)=e(z,v,\mu+\delta_{(x,s)}).

(E.73)

This means that the addition of
(x,s)
does not induce any changes to
\MalD_{(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mu)
and we deduce that

\MalD_{(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mu+\delta_{(x,s)})=\MalD_{(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mu)

(E.74)

and thus
\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mu)=0
.

Note that
R_{u}(y,A,\mu)
and the reasoning above only depend on
\mu_{|A\times[0,u)}
and thus for any finite
\chi\subset A\times(u,1]
, by the same reasoning one concludes that
R_{u}(y,A,\mu_{|A\times[0,u)}\cup\chi)=R_{u}(y,A,\mu_{|A\times[0,u)})=R_{u}(y,A,\mu)
and

\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mu_{|A\times[0,u)}\cup\chi)=0\qquad\text{if }|x-y|>3R_{u}(y,A,\mu).

(E.75)

In particular, this implies that for
(x,s),(y,u)\in tH\times[0,1]
with
s<u
and
|x-y|>3R_{u}(y,tH,\eta_{tH})
,

\mathbb{E}\big[\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\big|\eta_{|tH\times[0,u)}\big]=\int\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}(\eta_{|tH\times[0,u)}+\chi)\Pi_{u}(d\chi)=0,

(E.76)

where
\Pi_{u}
is the law of
\eta_{|tH\times(u,1]}
.

We conclude that

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{E}\big[\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\big|\eta_{|tH\times[0,u)}\big]\neq 0\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(|x-y|<3R_{u}(y,tH,\eta_{tH})\right).

(E.77)

As in the proof of Lemma
E.8
, there is a constant
c>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\tfrac{1}{3}|x-y|<R_{u}(y,tH,\eta_{tH})\right)\leqslant K\exp(-uc|x-y|^{d}),

(E.78)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark E.11
.

The proofs of Lemma
E.10
and the inequality (
E.4
) given in Proposition
E.1
build on and extend ideas used in
[
Wad09
]
. In particular, the proof of (
E.4
) adapts a rescaling argument from the proof of
[
Wad09
, Lemma 3.2]
and extends it to the Poisson setting and to arbitrary convex bodies. As already discussed, in our proof we need the fine control over constants introduced in Lemma
E.3
.

E.2
Moment estimates of add-one costs

To find the speed of convergence in the Kolmogorov distance, we need bounds on quantities of the type
\mathbb{E}|\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|^{r}
, with
r\geqslant 1
.

Proposition E.12
.

We work under the conditions of Theorem
5.3
. Let
\alpha>0
and
r\geqslant 1
. For every
\epsilon>0
and for all
t\geqslant 1
and all
(x,s),(y,u)\in tH\times(0,1]
with
s<u
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|^{r}\right]^{1/r}\leqslant c_{1}s^{-\alpha/d-\epsilon}

(E.79)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|^{r}\right]^{1/r}\leqslant c_{1}u^{-\alpha/d-\epsilon}

(E.80)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\MalD^{(2)}_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\neq 0\right)\leqslant C_{2}\exp(-c_{2}u|x-y|^{d}),

(E.81)

where
c_{1}>0
is a constant depending on
\alpha,r
and
\epsilon
and
c_{2},C_{2}>0
are absolute.

Remark E.13
.

The bound (
E.79
) is an extension to arbitrary exponents
r\geqslant 1
of what was shown in
[
Pen05
, Lemma 3.4]
. The proof below builds on the same arguments, but without using uniform random vectors.

Proof.

Proof of (
E.79
).
By (
E.26
) and (
E.35
), it can be seen that

|\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|^{r}\leqslant R_{s}(x)^{r\alpha}2^{r-1}\left(1+2^{r\alpha}|A(x,s)|^{r}\right).

(E.82)

where
A(x,s):=\{(y,u)\in\eta_{|tH\times(s,1]}:\ (y,u)\rightarrow(x,s)\text{ in }\eta_{tH}+\delta_{(x,s)}\}
is the set of points in
\eta_{tH}
that will connect to
(x,s)
upon addition of this point. Recall that any point connecting to
(x,s)
must be inside
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))
. For each
i=1,...,K
, define

A_{i}(x,s):=\{(y,u)\in\eta_{tH}\cap(C_{i}(x)\cap\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))\times(s,1]):\\
|x-y|<|x-z|\ \forall(z,v)\in\eta_{tH}\cap(C_{i}(x)\cap\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))\times(s,u))\}.

(E.83)

This is the set of points
(y,u)
in
\eta_{tH}
inside the intersection of the cone
C_{i}(x)
with
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))
that are closer to
x
than any other point of mark between
s
and
u
within this cone. Any point
(y,u)
connecting to
(x,s)
must be within such a set for some
i
, else there is a point closer to
y
than
x
and of lower mark, i.e. a potential neighbour. Hence

|A(x,s)|^{r}\leqslant\left(\sum_{i=1}^{K}|A_{i}(x,s)|\right)^{r}\leqslant K^{r-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{K}|A_{i}(x,s)|^{r}\right).

(E.84)

Define
m:=\lceil r\rceil
and fix
i\in\{1,...,K\}
. Since
|A_{i}(x,s)|
is a non-negative integer and
\frac{a}{m}\leqslant\frac{a-k}{m-k}
for
0\leqslant k\leqslant m-1
and
a\geqslant m
, we have

|A_{i}(x,s)|^{r}\leqslant|A_{i}(x,s)|^{m}\leqslant m^{m}{|A_{i}(x,s)|\choose m}+(m-1)^{m}.

(E.85)

Our goal is now to estimate
{|A_{i}(x,s)|\choose m}
. First, let

G_{i}:=\eta_{tH}\cap\left(C_{i}(x)\cap B^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))\times(s,1]\right)

(E.86)

be the set of points in
\eta_{tH}
that are closer than
R_{s}(x)
to
x
, within the cone
C_{i}(x)
and of mark higher than
s
. Any point connecting to
x
must be within this set. Let
N_{i}:=|G_{i}|
be the random number of points inside
G_{i}
and note that
N_{i}
is almost surely finite. Given
N_{i}
, the points in
G_{i}
can be denoted by the random coordinates
\{(y_{1},s_{1}),...,(y_{N_{i}},s_{N_{i}})\}
, where
y_{1},...,y_{N_{i}}
are the spatial coordinates of the points and
s_{1},...,s_{N_{i}}
are the marks of the points.

As
N_{i}
is almost surely finite, we can assume w.l.o.g. that the points
y_{1},...,y_{N_{i}}
are ordered by increasing distance to
x
. We now condition on the event
N_{i}=n\neq 0
and, given this conditioning, we also take conditional expectation with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the random coordinates
y_{j}
. Since
A_{i}(x,s)\subset G_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.{|A_{i}(x,s)|\choose m}\right|N_{i}=n,y_{1},...,y_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{z_{1},...,z_{m}\}\subset\{y_{1},...,y_{n}\}\\
\text{distinct}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1},...,z_{m}\in A_{i}(x,s)\}}\right|N_{i}=n,y_{1},...,y_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leqslant j_{1}<j_{2}<...<j_{m}\leqslant n}\mathbb{P}\left(\{y_{j_{1}},...,y_{j_{m}}\}\subset A_{i}(x,s)|N_{i}=n,y_{1},...,y_{n}\right)

(E.87)

Note that this expression is zero if
n<m
.

Conditional on the event
N_{i}=n
, the marks
s_{1},...,s_{n}
are independent of the spatial coordinates
y_{1},...,y_{n}
and i.i.d. uniformly distributed in
(s,1]
. Hence any ordering of the marks is equally likely. Conditional on the spatial coordinates
y_{1},...,y_{n}
, the event
\{y_{j_{1}},...,y_{j_{m}}\}\subset A_{i}(x,s)
happens if and only if
y_{j_{1}}
has the smallest mark among the points
y_{1},y_{2},...,y_{j_{1}}
, and
y_{j_{2}}
has the smallest mark among the points
y_{1},y_{2},...,y_{j_{2}}
etc. The probability that this happens is exactly given by
(j_{1}j_{2}...j_{m})^{-1}
. Thus

\mathbb{E}\left[\left.{|A_{i}(x,s)|\choose m}\right|N_{i}=n,y_{1},...,y_{n}\right]=\sum_{1\leqslant j_{1}<j_{2}<...<j_{m}\leqslant n}(j_{1}j_{2}...j_{m})^{-1}\leqslant\frac{1}{m!}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{j}\right)^{m}.

(E.88)

It holds that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{j}\leqslant\int_{1}^{n}\frac{1}{x}dx+1=\log(n)+1.

(E.89)

The bounds developed in (
E.85
)-(
E.89
) yield for
n\neq 0
:

\mathbb{E}\left[|A_{i}(x,s)|^{r}|N_{i}=n\right]\leqslant m^{m}\frac{1}{m!}(\log(n)+1)^{m}+(m-1)^{m}.

(E.90)

For any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, the function
g:[1,+\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:x\mapsto\left(m^{m}\frac{1}{m!}(\log(x)+1)^{m}+(m-1)^{m}\right)x^{-\epsilon^{\prime}}
is bounded by a constant
c>0
(dependent on
r
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
), implying that

\mathbb{E}\left[|A_{i}(x,s)|^{r}|N_{i}=n\right]\leqslant cn^{\epsilon^{\prime}}.

(E.91)

If
n=0
, then
|A_{i}(x,s)|=0
, hence the bound (
E.91
) continues to hold. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\left[|A_{i}(x,s)|^{r}|N_{i}\right]\leqslant cN_{i}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}.

(E.92)

Conditional on
\eta_{|tH\times[0,s)}
, the random variable
N_{i}
is equal in law to
\eta^{\prime}(tH\cap C_{i}(x)\cap\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))\times(s,1])
, where
\eta^{\prime}
is an independent copy of
\eta
. This quantity in turn is upper bounded by
\eta^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))\times[0,1])
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|A_{i}(x,s)|^{r}\big|\eta_{|tH\times[0,s)}\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant c\mathbb{E}\big[\eta^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,R_{s}(x))\times[0,1])^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\big|\eta_{|tH\times[0,s)}\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant c(\kappa_{d}R_{s}(x)^{d})^{\epsilon^{\prime}},

(E.93)

where we applied Jensen’s inequality to pass to the second inequality. Plugging this bound into (
E.85
), we deduce

\mathbb{E}\left[|A(x,s)|^{r}|\eta_{|tH\times[0,s)}\right]\leqslant cK^{r}\kappa_{d}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}R_{s}(x)^{\epsilon^{\prime}d}.

(E.94)

Combining (
E.94
) with (
E.82
) leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[|\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|^{r}\right]\leqslant c_{0}\mathbb{E}R_{s}(x)^{r\alpha}(1+R_{s}(x)^{\epsilon^{\prime}d}).

(E.95)

for some constant
c_{0}>0
. By Lemma
E.8
, the RHS is bounded by
c_{1}s^{-r\alpha/d-\epsilon^{\prime}}
for some constant
c_{1}>0
. For
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon r
, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[|\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|^{r}\right]^{1/r}\leqslant c_{1}^{1/r}s^{-\alpha/d-\epsilon}.

(E.96)

Proof of (
E.80
).
As in the proof of (
E.5
), we have

|\MalD_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}|\leqslant|\MalD_{(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\eta_{tH})|+R_{u}(y)^{\alpha}+\opL_{(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}(\eta_{tH})

(E.97)

and the result follows by the proof of (
E.79
).

Proof of (
E.81
).
As in the proof of (
E.6
), one see that if
|x-y|>3R_{u}(y)
, then

\MalD_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}=0.

(E.98)

Hence for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

\mathbb{P}(\MalD_{(x,s),(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\neq 0)\leqslant\mathbb{P}(|x-y|>3R_{u}(y))\leqslant c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}u|x-y|^{d}),

(E.99)

as seen in Lemma
E.8
.
∎

E.3
The orders of the variances

The goal of this subsection is to show the orders of the variances given in (
5.7
) and (
5.9
). For the sake of legibility, we will split the proof into several propositions.

Proposition E.14
.

There are constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that for
0<\alpha<\frac{d}{2}
,

\var(F^{(\alpha)}_{t})\leqslant c_{1}t^{d}

(E.100)

and

\var(F^{(d/2)}_{t})\leqslant c_{2}t^{d}\log t.

(E.101)

Remark E.15
.

The bounds in this proposition were already shown in
[
Wad09
, Theorem 2.1]
via proving the result for uniform vectors and subsequent Poissonisation. We include a proof for the sake of completeness. It is similar in spirit to the one given in
[
Wad09
]
, but works in a purely Poisson setting.

Proof.

Let
0<\alpha\leqslant\frac{d}{2}
. Since
F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\leqslant(\diam(H)t)^{\alpha}\eta(tH\times[0,1])
, it is clear that
F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\eta})
. Hence by Lemma
A.3
, we have

\var\left(F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\right)=\mathbb{E}\int_{tH}\int_{0}^{1}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\MalD_{(y,u)}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\big|\eta_{|tH\times[0,u)}\big]^{2}\,dydu.

(E.102)

Applying (
E.48
) to the RHS of (
E.102
) yields

\var\left(F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\right)\leqslant c\ \mathbb{E}\int_{tH}\int_{0}^{1}\,R_{u}(y,tH,\eta_{t}H)^{2\alpha}\,dydu

(E.103)

for some constant
c>0
. Now applying Lemma
E.8
to the RHS of (
E.103
) gives

\var\left(F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\right)\leqslant c^{\prime}\int_{tH}\int_{0}^{1}\,\left(t^{2\alpha}\wedge u^{-2\alpha/d}\right)dydu

(E.104)

for some other constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Integrating now yields the result.
∎

Proposition E.16
.

For
\alpha>0
, there is a constant
c>0
such that

ct^{d}\leqslant\var\left(F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\right).

(E.105)

The proof will make use of
[
LPS16
, Theorem 5.2]
.

Proof of Prop.
E.16
.

Recall that there are
y_{0}\in H
and
\delta>0
such that
B^{d}(y_{0},\delta)\subset H
, as defined at the beginning of this Section
E
. Also recall the definition of the cones
C_{1}^{+}(0),...,C_{K}^{+}(0)
from section
E.1
. For
i\in\{1,...,K\}
, define

V_{i}:=C_{i}^{+}(0)\cap B^{d}(0,1)\setminus B^{d}\big(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\big).

(E.106)

Now for
0<\tau<\frac{\delta}{2}
, define

U_{\tau}:=\bigg\{(x,s,x+\tau z_{1},s_{1},...,x+\tau z_{K},s_{K}):(x,s)\in tB^{d}\left(y_{0},\tfrac{\delta}{2}\right)\times\left[\tfrac{1}{2},1\right],\\
\text{ and }(z_{i},s_{i})\in V_{i}\times\big[0,\tfrac{1}{2}\big),\ i=1,...,K\bigg\}.

(E.107)

Note that
U_{\tau}\subset(tH\times[0,1])^{K+1}
. For
[
LPS16
, Theorem 5.2]
to yield a lower bound as in (
E.105
), we need to show that for a suitably chosen
\tau
to be defined later,

1.

There is a constant
c_{1}>0
such that for all
(x,s,\tilde{z}_{1},s_{1},...,\tilde{z}_{K},s_{K})\in U_{\tau}
,

\left|\mathbb{E}F^{(\alpha)}\left(\eta_{tH}+\delta_{(x,s)}+\sum_{i=1}^{K}\delta_{(\tilde{z}_{i},s_{i})}\right)-F^{(\alpha)}\left(\eta_{tH}+\sum_{i=1}^{K}\delta_{(\tilde{z}_{i},s_{i})}\right)\right|\geqslant c_{1}.

(E.108)

2.

There is a constant
c_{2}>0
such that

\min_{\emptyset\neq J\subset\{1,...,K+1\}}\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}V\subset U_{\tau}\\
\lambda^{(K+1)}(V)\geqslant\lambda^{(K+1)}(U_{\tau})/2^{K+2}\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(|J|)}\left(\Pi_{J}(V)\right)\geqslant c_{2}t^{d}.

(E.109)

Proof of
1
.
We use a construction similar to the one in
[
LPS16
, Lemma 7.1]
for the
k
-Nearest Neighbour graph. Let
(x,s)\in tB^{d}\left(y_{0},\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\times\left[\frac{1}{2},1\right]
and
(z_{i},s_{i})\in V_{i}\times\left[\left.0,\frac{1}{2}\right)\right.
for all
i=1,...,K
. Define

\mathcal{A}_{\tau}:=\eta_{tH}+\sum_{i=1}^{K}\delta_{(x+\tau z_{i},s_{i})}.

(E.110)

By the choice of the points
z_{1},...,z_{K}
, we infer that

R_{s}(x,tH,\mathcal{A}_{\tau})\leqslant\tau

(E.111)

and no point outside
B^{d}(x,\tau)
will connect to
x
, since there is always a point
z_{i}
which is closer (by Lemma 3.3 in
[
Pen05
]
).

If
\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])=0
, then no point at all will connect to
x
and the only change upon addition of
x
is the addition of the edge from
x
to its online nearest neighbour. Since there is no point of
\eta
in
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)
, the online nearest neighbour of
x
must be one of the points
z_{1},...,z_{K}
. But
|x-z_{i}|\geqslant\frac{\tau}{2}
for all
i=1,...,K
and we deduce

\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})\geqslant 2^{-\alpha}\tau^{\alpha}.

(E.112)

If
\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])\neq 0
, we use that by (
E.26
) and the proof of Lemma
E.10
,

\displaystyle|\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})|

\displaystyle\leqslant e(x,s,\mathcal{A}_{\tau})^{\alpha}+\opL_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})

\displaystyle\leqslant R_{s}(x,tH,\mathcal{A}_{\tau})^{\alpha}+(2R_{s}(x,tH,\mathcal{A}_{\tau}))^{\alpha}\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])

\displaystyle\leqslant\tau^{\alpha}+(2\tau)^{\alpha}\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])

(E.113)

to find the following bound:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])\neq 0\}}\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}(\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])\neq 0)\tau^{\alpha}+(2\tau)^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\eta(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muB\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])

\displaystyle=\tau^{\alpha}(1-\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}))+(2\tau)^{\alpha}\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}.

(E.114)

Combining (
E.112
) and (
E.114
), we find

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geqslant\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(B^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])=0\}}\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})-\left|\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(B^{d}(x,\tau)\times[0,1])\neq 0\}}\MalD_{(x,s)}F^{(\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}_{\tau})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geqslant 2^{-\alpha}\tau^{\alpha}\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d})-\tau^{\alpha}(1-\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}))-(2\tau)^{\alpha}\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\tau^{\alpha}\left(\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d})2^{-\alpha}-(1-\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}))-2^{\alpha}\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}\right)=:c_{\tau}.

(E.115)

But we have

\lim_{\tau\rightarrow 0}\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d})2^{-\alpha}-(1-\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}))-2^{\alpha}\kappa_{d}\tau^{d}=2^{-\alpha}>0,

(E.116)

which means that we can choose
\tau>0
small enough such that
c_{\tau}>0
. This choice of
\tau
depends only on
\alpha
and
d
. We fix this
\tau
for the rest of the proof.

Proof of
2
.
We follow the same type of reasoning as was used in the proof of
[
LPS16
, Theorem 5.3]
. First, note that

|U_{\tau}|=\kappa_{d}2^{-d-K-1}\delta^{d}|V_{1}|^{K}t^{d}.

(E.117)

Let
\emptyset\neq J=\{i_{1},...,i_{|J|}\}\subset\{1,...,K+1\}
. For any
(y,u)=(y_{1},u_{1},...,y_{K+1},u_{K+1})\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]\right)^{K+1}
, write
(y,u)_{J}=(y_{i_{1}},u_{i_{1}},...,y_{i_{|J|}},u_{i_{|J|}})
. If
(y,u)\in U
, then for any
i,j\in\{1,...,K+1\}
, we have that
y_{j}\in B^{d}(y_{i},2\tau)
and
u_{j}\in\left[\left.0,\frac{1}{2}\right)\right.
. This implies that for any
(y,u)_{J}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]\right)^{|J|}
,

\lambda^{(K+1-|J|)}\left((y,u)_{J^{c}}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]\right)^{K+1-|J|}:\left((y,u)_{J},(y,u)_{J^{c}}\right)\in U\right)\leqslant\left(2^{d-1}\tau^{d}\kappa_{d}\right)^{K+1-|J|},

(E.118)

where we use
\lambda
for Lebesgue measure. Thus for any
V\subset U
,

\displaystyle\lambda^{(K+1)}(V)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]\right)^{K+1}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{(y,u)_{J}\in\Pi_{J}(V)\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{((y,u)_{J},(y,u)_{J^{c}})\in U\}}\lambda^{(K+1)}(d(y,u))

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(2^{d-1}\tau^{d}\kappa_{d}\right)^{K+1-|J|}\lambda^{(|J|)}\left(\Pi_{J}(V)\right).

(E.119)

Hence any
V\subset U
with
\lambda^{(K+1)}(V)\geqslant\lambda^{(K+1)}(U)2^{-(K+2)}
satisfies

\lambda^{(|J|)}\left(\Pi_{J}(V)\right)\geqslant\left(2^{d-1}\tau^{d}\kappa_{d}\right)^{-(K+1-|J|)}2^{-(K+2)}\kappa_{d}2^{-d-K-1}\delta^{d}|V_{1}|^{K}t^{d}.

(E.120)

This in turn is lower bounded by
c_{2}t^{d}
, where

c_{2}:=\min\left\{1,\left(2^{d-1}\tau^{d}\kappa_{d}\right)^{-K}\right\}\kappa_{d}2^{-d-2K-3}\delta^{d}|V_{1}|^{K}.

(E.121)

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition E.17
.

There are constants
c>0
and
T_{0}\geqslant 1
such that for all
t\geqslant T_{0}
,

ct^{d}\log(t)\leqslant\var\left(F^{(d/2)}_{t}\right).

(E.122)

Remark E.18
.

By inspection of the arguments in Lemmas
E.19
-
E.25
, one sees that

d\left(1-\tfrac{\pi}{2}+c(d)\right)\leqslant\liminf_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\var\left(F^{(d/2)}_{t}\right)}{t^{d}\log(t)},

(E.123)

where
c(d)
is a positive constant such that
c(d)>\frac{\pi}{2}-1
for
d\geqslant 1
. We believe this bound to correspond to the exact asymptotic order of
\var\left(F^{(d/2)}_{t}\right)
. We can however only provide a closed form of
c(d)
in dimension
d=1
. For dimension
d=2
, we numerically estimate
c(2)
and for dimensions
d\geqslant 3
, we use a lower bound which is smaller than the LHS of (
E.123
):

d\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}-\tilde{c}(d)\right)\leqslant\liminf_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\var\left(F^{(d/2)}_{t}\right)}{t^{d}\log(t)},

(E.124)

where
0<\tilde{c}(d)<1-\frac{\pi}{4}
for
d\geqslant 3
and
\tilde{c}(d)\rightarrow 0
as
d\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma E.19
.

Let
\alpha>0
and
t\geqslant 1
. Let
\ell:=\diam(H)
. Then

\var(F^{(\alpha)}_{t})=I_{1}(t)+I_{2}(t)-I_{3}(t)+I_{4}(t)-I_{5}(t),

(E.125)

where the terms are defined as follows:

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{2\alpha}}ds\left(\exp(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/(2\alpha)})|)-\exp(-v|tH|)\right)

\displaystyle I_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|tH\cap B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})|\right)\exp\left(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left[\exp\left(u|tH\cap B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\right)-1\right]

\displaystyle I_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|tH\cap B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})|\right)\exp\left(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\right)

\displaystyle I_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u+v\geqslant 1\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|tH\cap B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})|\right)\exp\left(-v|tH|\right)

\displaystyle I_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\exp\left(-u|tH|\right)\exp\left(-v|tH|\right)

Proof.

We start by pointing out a few identities. The functional
F^{(\alpha)}_{t}
can be written

F^{(\alpha)}_{t}=\int_{tH\times[0,1]}e(y,v,\eta)^{\alpha}\eta(dy,dv).

(E.126)

Moreover, for
(y,v),(x,u)\in tH\times[0,1]
with
u<v
, we have

e(y,v,\eta)^{\alpha}=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,v))\neq 0\}}\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})\times[0,v))=0\}}

(E.127)

and

e\left(y,v,\eta+\delta_{(x,u)}\right)^{\alpha}=|x-y|^{\alpha}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,v))=0\}}\\
+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,v))\neq 0\}}\int_{0}^{|x-y|^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})\times[0,v))=0\}}.

(E.128)

Note also that
e(y,v,\eta)=e(y,v,\eta_{|tH\times[0,v)})
, a fact we are going to use repeatedly.

Combining (
E.126
) and (
E.127
) with Mecke equation (
2.6
) allows us now to calculate
\mathbb{E}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}F^{(\alpha)}_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\ \mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,v))\neq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,r^{1/\alpha})\times[0,v))=0\}}

\displaystyle=\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\ \left(\exp\big(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,r^{1/\alpha})|\big)-\exp(-v|tH|)\right).

(E.129)

To calculate
\mathbb{E}\big[(F^{(\alpha)}_{t})^{2}\big]
, note first that

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{tH\times[0,1]}e(y,v,\eta)^{\alpha}\eta(dy,dv)\right)^{2}\\
=\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\ \mathbb{E}\ e(y,v,\eta)^{2\alpha}+\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\ \mathbb{E}\ e(y,v,\eta+\delta_{(x,u)})^{\alpha}e(x,u,\eta+\delta_{(y,v)})^{\alpha},

(E.130)

as can be seen by applying Mecke equation twice or other means. The first term on the RHS of (
E.130
) is equal to
\mathbb{E}\big[F_{t}^{(2\alpha)}\big]
, which by (
E.129
) is equal to
I_{1}(t)
.

The second term on the RHS of (
E.130
) can by symmetry be written as

2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbb{E}\ e(y,v,\eta+\delta_{(x,u)})^{\alpha}e(x,u,\eta)^{\alpha}.

(E.131)

Plugging in (
E.127
) and (
E.128
) and using that the product
\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,u))\neq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,v))=0\}}
is zero, leads to

2\mathbb{E}\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}\\
\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\times[0,u))\neq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})\times[0,u))=0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})\times[0,v))=0\}}.

(E.132)

Writing

\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})\times[0,v))=0\}}=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})\times[0,u))=0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta(tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})\times[u,v))=0\}}

(E.133)

and taking expectation, using that
\eta_{|tH\times[0,u)}
and
\eta_{|tH\times[u,v)}
are independent, (
E.132
) is equal to

2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}\exp\left(-(v-u)|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\right)\\
\left(\exp\big(-u|tH\cap(B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})\cup B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha}))|\big)-\exp(-u|tH|)\right).

(E.134)

Combining (
E.134
) with (
E.129
), we have

\var\left(F^{(\alpha)}_{t}\right)=I_{1}(t)+A(t)+B(t)+C(t)

(E.135)

with

\displaystyle A(t)=2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-(v-u)|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\right)\exp\big(-u|tH\cap(B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})\cup B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha}))|\big)

\displaystyle B(t)=-2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-(v-u)|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\right)\exp(-u|tH|)

\displaystyle C(t)=-\left(\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \left(\exp\big(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|\big)-\exp(-v|tH|)\right)\right)^{2}

It can easily be seen that

C(t)=C_{1}(t)+C_{2}(t)-I_{3}(t)-I_{5}(t)

(E.136)

with

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|)\exp(-u|tH\cap B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})|)

\displaystyle C_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \exp(-v|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})|)\exp(-u|tH|)

It remains to show that
I_{2}(t)+I_{4}(t)=A(t)+B(t)+C_{1}(t)+C_{2}(t)
. Let us first show that
A(t)+C_{1}(t)=I_{2}(t)
. Indeed, denote
R:=B^{d}(x,r^{1/\alpha})
and
S:=B^{d}(y,s^{1/\alpha})
. Then

\exp(-(v-u)|tH\cap S|)\exp(-u|tH\cap(S\cup R)|)\\
=\exp(-v|tH\cap S|)\exp(-u|tH\cap R|)\exp(u|tH\cap S\cap R|)

(E.137)

and hence

A(t)+C_{1}(t)=2\int_{tH}dx\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}dr\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{\alpha}}ds\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u<v\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<|x-y|^{\alpha}\}}\\
\exp(-v|tH\cap S|)\exp(-u|tH\cap R|)\left(\exp(u|tH\cap S\cap R|)-1\right)=I_{2}(t).

(E.138)

To see that
B(t)+C_{2}(t)=I_{4}(t)
, it suffices to perform a change of variables in
B(t)
by taking
\tilde{v}:=v-u
,
d\tilde{v}=dv
.
∎

In the following we are going to deal with the different terms in Lemma
E.19
in the case
\alpha=\frac{d}{2}
.

Lemma E.20
.

In the notation of Lemma
E.19
and with
\alpha=\frac{d}{2}
,

I_{1}(t)\geqslant|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}t^{d}\log(t^{d})+O(t^{d}).

(E.139)

Proof.

First, we show that the second part of the expression
I_{1}(t)
is
O(t^{d})
. Indeed,

\int_{tH}dy\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{d}}ds\exp(-v|tH|)=t^{d}\ell^{d}(1-\exp(-t^{d}|H|))=O(t^{d}).

(E.140)

Note that since
|tH\cap B^{d}(y,s^{1/d})|\leqslant\kappa_{d}s
, the first part of the expression
I_{1}(t)
is lower bounded by

\int_{tH}dy\int_{t^{-d}}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{(t\ell)^{d}}ds\ \exp(-v\kappa_{d}s),

(E.141)

which after integrating in
y
and
s
and the change of variables
u=t^{d}v
, is equal to

t^{d}|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}\left(\log(t^{d})-\int_{1}^{t^{d}}du\ u^{-1}\exp(-u\kappa_{d}\ell^{d})\right).

(E.142)

Since
\int_{1}^{\infty}du\ u^{-1}\exp(-u\kappa_{d}\ell^{d})<\infty
, this term is equal to
t^{d}|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}\log(t^{d})+O(t^{d})
, concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma E.21
.

In the notation of Lemma
E.19
and with
\alpha=\frac{d}{2}
, there is a constant
T_{0}\geqslant 1
such that for all
t\geqslant T_{0}
, we have

I_{3}(t)\leqslant|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}\frac{\pi}{2}t^{d}\log(t^{d})+O(t^{d}).

(E.143)

See the proof of
[
Wad09
, Lemma 3.6]
for a similar computation to the one done below.

Proof.

Start with a change of variables:

\begin{cases}\tilde{x}=t^{-1}x,\ dx=t^{d}d\tilde{x}\\
\tilde{y}=t^{-1}y,\ dy=t^{d}d\tilde{y}\\
\tilde{s}=t^{-d/2}s,\ ds=t^{d/2}d\tilde{s}\\
\tilde{r}=t^{-d/2}r,\ dr=t^{d/2}d\tilde{r}\\
\tilde{u}=t^{d}u,\ du=t^{-d}d\tilde{u}\\
\tilde{v}=t^{d}v,\ dv=t^{-d}d\tilde{v},\end{cases}

(E.144)

which leads to

I_{3}(t)=2t^{d}\int_{H}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{0}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}ds\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{d/2}\}}\\
\exp\left(-u|H\cap B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})|\right)\exp\left(-v|H\cap B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})|\right).

(E.145)

We can reduce the integration interval of the variable
v
to
[\tau_{0},t^{d}]
, for some large constant
\tau_{0}
and large enough
t
, since the rest term is clearly
O(t^{d})
. For any
v\geqslant\tau_{0}
, we then have
|H\setminus H_{2v^{-1/(2d)}}|\leqslant\beta_{H}2v^{-1/(2d)}
by the discussion at the start of Section
5.1
.

We can now split the above expression as follows:

\displaystyle I_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=2t^{d}\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{H}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}ds\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{d/2}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|H\cap B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})|\right)\exp\left(-v|H\cap B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})|\right)

\displaystyle+2t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{H\setminus H_{2v^{-1/(2d)}}}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}ds\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{d/2}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|H\cap B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})|\right)\exp\left(-v|H\cap B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})|\right)

\displaystyle+2t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{H_{2v^{-1/(2d)}}}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{v^{-1/4}}^{\ell^{d/2}}ds\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{d/2}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|H\cap B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})|\right)\exp\left(-v|H\cap B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})|\right)

\displaystyle+2t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{H_{2v^{-1/(2d)}}}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{0}^{v^{-1/4}}ds\int_{v^{-1/4}}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{d/2}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|H\cap B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})|\right)\exp\left(-v|H\cap B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})|\right)

\displaystyle+2t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{H_{2v^{-1/(2d)}}}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{0}^{v^{-1/4}}ds\int_{0}^{v^{-1/4}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s>|x-y|^{d/2}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-u|H\cap B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})|\right)\exp\left(-v|H\cap B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})|\right)

\displaystyle=O(t^{d})+R_{1}(t)+R_{2}(t)+R_{3}(t)+I_{3}^{\prime}(t)

(E.146)

Note that in
I_{3}^{\prime}(t)
, due to the choice of sets we are integrating over, we have
B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})\subset H
and
B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})\subset H
. Hence
I_{3}^{\prime}(t)
is upper bounded by

2t^{d}\int_{1}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\int_{H}dx\int_{B^{d}(x,s^{2/d})}dy\ \exp\left(-u\kappa_{d}r^{2}\right)\exp\left(-v\kappa_{d}r^{2}\right)

(E.147)

which is equal to
|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}\frac{\pi}{2}t^{d}\log(t^{d})
. Hence

I_{3}^{\prime}(t)\leqslant|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}\tfrac{\pi}{2}t^{d}\log(t^{d}).

(E.148)

It remains to show that the three rest terms
R_{1}(t),R_{2}(t),R_{3}(t)
are
O(t^{d})
. To this end, recall from Lemma
E.3
that there is a constant
c_{H}>0
such that
|H\cap B^{d}(w,s)|\geqslant c_{H}s^{d}
for any
w\in H
and
s\leqslant\ell
. This implies that

R_{1}(t)\leqslant 2t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\int_{H\setminus H_{2v^{-1/(2d)}}}dx\int_{B^{d}(x,s^{2/d})}dy\ \exp\left(-uc_{H}r^{2}\right)\exp\left(-vc_{H}s^{2}\right)

(E.149)

which in turn is upper bounded by

t^{d}\pi\kappa_{d}c_{H}^{-2}\beta_{H}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}v^{-1-1/(2d)}=O(t^{d}).

(E.150)

By the same reasoning, we get

\displaystyle R_{2}(t)\leqslant 2|H|\kappa_{d}t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{v^{-1/4}}^{\infty}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ s^{2}\exp\left(-uc_{H}r^{2}\right)\exp\left(-vc_{H}s^{2}\right)

(E.151)

and

\displaystyle R_{3}(t)\leqslant 2|H|\kappa_{d}t^{d}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{v^{-1/4}}^{\infty}dr\ s^{2}\exp\left(-uc_{H}r^{2}\right)\exp\left(-vc_{H}s^{2}\right).

(E.152)

both of which can be shown to be
O(t^{d})
.
∎

Lemma E.22
.

In the notation of Lemma
E.19
and with
\alpha=\frac{d}{2}
, one has
I_{4}(t)=O(t^{d})
and
I_{5}(t)=O(t^{d})
.

Remark E.23
.

Since
I_{4}(t)\geqslant 0
, it is not necessary to calculate the order of this term to find a lower bound of
\var(F^{(d/2)}_{t})
. However, we include the proof for completeness.

Proof.

Perform the same change of variables and upper bound as in the proof of Lemma
E.21
to find

I_{4}(t)\leqslant 2t^{d}\int_{H}dx\int_{H}dy\int_{0}^{t^{d}}dv\int_{0}^{t^{d}}du\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}ds\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{u+v\geqslant t^{d}\}}\\
\exp\left(-uc_{H}r^{2}\right)\exp\left(-v|H|\right).

(E.153)

Integrating over
x
,
y
,
s
and
v
yields the upper bound

I_{4}(t)\leqslant 2|H|\ell^{d/2}t^{d}\int_{0}^{t^{d}}du\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \exp(-uc_{H}r^{2})\left(\exp(-(t^{d}-u)|H|)-\exp(-t^{d}|H|)\right).

(E.154)

The integrand is bounded by
\exp(-(t^{d}-u)|H|)
. Introducing the change of variable
\tilde{u}=t^{d}-u
yields the upper bound

I_{4}(t)\leqslant 2|H|\ell^{d/2}t^{d}\int_{0}^{t^{d}}du\int_{0}^{\ell^{d/2}}dr\ \exp(-u|H|)\leqslant 2\ell^{d}t^{d}=O(t^{d}).

(E.155)

As for
I_{5}(t)
, integrating over all variables yields that

I_{5}(t)=t^{d}\ell^{d}(1-\exp(-t|H|))^{2}=O(t^{d}).

(E.156)

∎

Lemma E.24

(Joint work with Pierre Perruchaud)
.

In the notation of Lemma
E.19
and with
\alpha=\frac{d}{2}
, for every
\epsilon>0
, there is a constant
T_{0}\geqslant 1
such that for all
t\geqslant T_{0}
,

I_{2}(t)\geqslant(|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}c(d)-\epsilon)t^{d}\log(t^{d})+O(t^{d}),

(E.157)

where

c(d)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|z|\geqslant 1\}}\\
\left(\left(|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cup B^{d}(z,1)|\right)^{-1}-\kappa_{d}^{-1}(r^{2}+1)^{-1}\right).

(E.158)

Proof.

Fix
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
such that
(|H|-\tilde{\epsilon})(c(d)-\tilde{\epsilon})\geqslant|H|c(d)-\epsilon
and let
\tilde{\delta}>0
be such that
\beta_{H}\tilde{\delta}<\tilde{\epsilon}
and
|H_{\tilde{\delta}}|\geqslant|H|-\tilde{\epsilon}
(which is possible by property (
E.3
)).

Assume that
x\in tH_{\tilde{\delta}}
,
y\in B^{d}(x,\frac{t\tilde{\delta}}{2})
,
r\leqslant(t\tilde{\delta})^{d/2}
and
s\leqslant|x-y|^{d/2}
. Then
B^{d}(x,t\tilde{\delta})\subset tH
,
B^{d}(x,r^{2/d})\subset tH
and
B^{d}(y,s^{2/d})\subset tH
. In the integrals making up
I_{2}(t)
, we can reduce the intervals of integration to the ones stated here and hence
I_{2}(t)
is lower bounded by

2\int_{tH_{\tilde{\delta}}}dx\int_{B^{d}(x,\frac{t\tilde{\delta}}{2})}dy\int_{0}^{(t\tilde{\delta})^{d/2}}dr\int_{0}^{|x-y|^{d/2}}ds\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{v}du\\
\exp(-u\kappa_{d}r^{2})\exp(-v\kappa_{d}s^{2})\left(\exp(u|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(y-x,s^{2/d})|)-1\right).

(E.159)

Now carry out the following changes of variables:

\begin{cases}\tilde{u}=v^{-1}u,\ d\tilde{u}=v^{-1}du\\
z=v^{1/d}(y-x),\ dz=vdy\\
\tilde{s}=v^{1/2}s,\ d\tilde{s}=v^{1/2}ds\\
\tilde{r}=v^{1/2}r,\ d\tilde{r}=v^{1/2}dr\end{cases}

(E.160)

and deduce that the above expression is equal to

2\int_{0}^{1}dv\int_{0}^{1}d\tilde{u}\int_{tH_{\tilde{\delta}}}dx\int_{B^{d}(0,tv^{1/d}\tilde{\delta}/2)}dz\int_{0}^{(tv^{1/d}\tilde{\delta})^{d/2}}d\tilde{r}\int_{0}^{|z|^{d/2}}d\tilde{s}\\
v^{-1}\exp(-\tilde{u}\kappa_{d}\tilde{r}^{2})\exp(-\kappa_{d}\tilde{s}^{2})\left(\exp(\tilde{u}|B^{d}(0,\tilde{r}^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,\tilde{s}^{2/d})|)-1\right)

(E.161)

Assume now that
t\geqslant\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\tau_{0}=:T_{0}
for some
\tau_{0}>0
and lower bound this expression by reducing to the integration interval where
v^{1/d}\geqslant t^{-1}\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\tau_{0}
. Integrating additionally over
x
, we get the lower bound

t^{d}|H_{\tilde{\delta}}|\left(\int_{(t^{-1}\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\tau_{0})^{d}}^{1}dv\ v^{-1}\right)2\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{B^{d}(0,\tau_{0}/2)}dz\int_{0}^{\tau_{0}^{d/2}}dr\int_{0}^{|z|^{d/2}}ds\\
\exp(-u\kappa_{d}r^{2})\exp(-\kappa_{d}s^{2})\left(\exp(u|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,s^{2/d})|)-1\right).

(E.162)

For
\tau_{0}
large enough, this is lower bounded by

t^{d}(|H|-\tilde{\epsilon})(\log(t^{d})-d\log(\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\tau_{0}))(c_{0}(d)-\tilde{\epsilon})\geqslant(|H|c_{0}(d)-\epsilon)t^{d}\log(t^{d})+O(t^{d})

(E.163)

with

c_{0}(d):=2\int_{0}^{1}du\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\int_{0}^{|z|^{d/2}}ds\exp(-u\kappa_{d}r^{2})\exp(-\kappa_{d}s^{2})\\
\left(\exp(u|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,s^{2/d})|)-1\right).

(E.164)

The last thing that remains to do is to show that
c_{0}(d)=\kappa_{d}^{-1}c(d)
. Perform the successive change of variables
\tilde{r}=u^{1/2}r,\ \tilde{z}=u^{1/d}z,\ \tilde{s}=u^{1/2}s
and
\tilde{u}=u^{-1}
, then integrate over
\tilde{u}
to get

c_{0}(d)=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\int_{0}^{|z|^{d/2}}ds\ \kappa_{d}^{-1}s^{-2}\exp(-\kappa_{d}s^{2})\exp(-\kappa_{d}r^{2})\\
\left(\exp(|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,s^{2/d})|)-1\right).

(E.165)

Then change variables
\tilde{z}=s^{-2/d}z
and
\tilde{r}=s^{-1}r
and integrate over
s
to find
c_{0}(d)=\kappa_{d}^{-1}c(d)
.
∎

Combining Lemmas
E.20
,
E.21
,
E.22
and
E.24
, we have shown that for every
\epsilon>0
, there is a
T_{0}\geqslant 1
such that for all
t\geqslant T_{0}
,

\var\left(F^{(d/2)}_{t}\right)\geqslant t^{d}\log(t^{d})\left[|H|\kappa_{d}^{-1}\left(1+c(d)-\frac{\pi}{2}\right)-\epsilon\right]+O(t^{d}).

(E.166)

It remains thus to show that for
d\in\mathbb{N}
,

c(d)>\frac{\pi}{2}-1\approx 0.57,

(E.167)

which will be shown in the following lemma.

Lemma E.25

(Joint work with Pierre Perruchaud)
.

In the notation of Lemma
E.24
, for
d\in\mathbb{N}
,

c(d)>\frac{\pi}{2}-1\approx 0.57.

(E.168)

Proof.

We start by showing a lower bound on
c(d)
. Indeed, for all
x\in(0,1)
,

\frac{1}{1-x}-1=\frac{x}{1-x}\geqslant x.

(E.169)

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cup B^{d}(z,1)|}-\frac{1}{\kappa_{d}(r^{2}+1)}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{d}^{-1}(r^{2}+1)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{1-\kappa_{d}^{-1}(r^{2}+1)^{-1}|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,1)|}-1\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\kappa_{d}^{-2}(r^{2}+1)^{-2}|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,1)|.

(E.170)

A lower bound for
c(d)
is thus given by

\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ \kappa_{d}^{-2}(r^{2}+1)^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|z|\geqslant 1\}}|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,1)|.

(E.171)

Looking only at the inner integral over
z
, note that we can rewrite it as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|z|\geqslant 1\}}|B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})\cap B^{d}(z,1)|

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dx\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|z|\geqslant 1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leqslant r^{2/d}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x-z|\leqslant 1\}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dx\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leqslant r^{2/d}\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dz\ \left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x-z|\leqslant 1\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x-z|\leqslant 1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|z|\leqslant 1\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})}dx\ \left(\kappa_{d}-|B^{d}(0,1)\cap B^{d}(x,1)|\right).

(E.172)

Plugging this into the lower bound (
E.171
), we obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ \kappa_{d}^{-2}(r^{2}+1)^{-2}\int_{B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})}dx\ \left(\kappa_{d}-|B^{d}(0,1)\cap B^{d}(x,1)|\right),

(E.173)

which can be written as

\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ (r^{2}+1)^{-2}r^{2}-\tilde{c}(d)=\frac{\pi}{4}-\tilde{c}(d)

(E.174)

with

\tilde{c}(d):=\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ \kappa_{d}^{-2}(r^{2}+1)^{-2}\int_{B^{d}(0,r^{2/d})}dx\ |B^{d}(0,1)\cap B^{d}(x,1)|.

(E.175)

Hence
c(d)\geqslant\frac{\pi}{4}-\tilde{c}(d)
and to show that
c(d)\geqslant\frac{\pi}{2}-1
, it suffices to show that
\tilde{c}(d)\leqslant 1-\frac{\pi}{4}\approx 0.215
. First, note that

|B^{d}(0,1)\cap B^{d}(x,1)|=2\kappa_{d-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leqslant 2\}}\int_{\frac{|x|}{2}}^{1}dy\ (1-y^{2})^{\frac{d-1}{2}}.

(E.176)

which can be seen by integrating over the
d-1
-dimensional hyperspheres making up the spherical caps of the intersection, or by following the development in
[
Li11
]
.
Hence
\tilde{c}(d)
can be written

\displaystyle\tilde{c}(d)

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{-2}\int_{0}^{1}dy\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dx\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leqslant r^{2/d}\wedge 2y\}}(r^{2}+1)^{-2}(1-y^{2})^{\frac{d-1}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}dy\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ \left(r^{2}\wedge(2y)^{d}\right)(r^{2}+1)^{-2}(1-y^{2})^{\frac{d-1}{2}},

(E.177)

where we integrated over
x
in the second line. For any
u\geqslant 0
, define

g(u):=2\int_{0}^{\infty}dr\ (r^{2}\wedge u^{2})(r^{2}+1)^{-2}=\frac{\pi}{2}u^{2}+(1-u^{2})\arctan(u)-u.

(E.178)

Then it can be verified by standard methods that
g(0)=0
, as well as
\lim_{u\rightarrow\infty}g(u)=\frac{\pi}{2}
and the function is strictly increasing. Moreover,
g^{\prime}(u)\leqslant\pi u
, therefore
g(x)\leqslant\frac{\pi}{2}x^{2}
.

We now have

\tilde{c}(d)=\kappa_{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ (1-y^{2})^{\frac{d-1}{2}}g\left((2y)^{d/2}\right).

(E.179)

Change variables
u=\frac{1-y}{2}
to get

\tilde{c}(d)=2^{d}\kappa_{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{-1}\int_{0}^{1/2}du\ u^{\frac{d-1}{2}}(1-u)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}g\left(2^{d/2}(1-2u)^{d/2}\right)

(E.180)

and note that

2^{d}\kappa_{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{-1}\int_{0}^{x}du\ u^{\frac{d-1}{2}}(1-u)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}=I_{x}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right),

(E.181)

where
I_{x}(a,b)
is the regularized incomplete beta function (see
[
Par
]
for details).

Let us now deal with dimensions
d\in\{3,...,9\}
. Split the integration interval
[0,\frac{1}{2}]
into intervals
[\frac{i-1}{40},\frac{i}{40}]
for
i\in\{1,2,...,20\}
and upper bound
g\left(2^{d/2}(1-2u)^{d/2}\right)
by
g\left(2^{d/2}(1-\frac{i-1}{10})^{d/2}\right)
for
u\in(\frac{i-1}{40},\frac{i}{40}]
. We deduce the following bound:

\tilde{c}(d)\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{20}g\left(\left(2-\frac{i-1}{10}\right)^{d/2}\right)\left(I_{\frac{i}{40}}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right)-I_{\frac{i-1}{40}}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right)\right)=:\beta_{1}(d)

(E.182)

This results in the values in Table
1
, all of which are smaller than
1-\frac{\pi}{4}\approx 0.215
.

d

\beta_{1}(d)

3

0.203

4

0.175

5

0.150

6

0.128

7

0.110

8

0.094

9

0.081

Table 1:
Values of
\beta_{1}(d)

For
d\geqslant 10
, take
\theta=0.32
and split
\tilde{c}(d)
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{c}(d)

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}{}=2^{d}\kappa_{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{-1}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\theta}du\ u^{\frac{d-1}{2}}(1-u)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\underbrace{g\left(2^{d/2}(1-2u)^{d/2}\right)}_{\leqslant\frac{\pi}{2}}\\
+\int_{\theta}^{1/2}du\ u^{\frac{d-1}{2}}(1-u)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\underbrace{g\left(2^{d/2}(1-2u)^{d/2}\right)}_{\leqslant\pi 2^{d-1}(1-2\theta)^{d}}\bigg)\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\pi}{2}I_{\theta}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right)+\pi 2^{d-1}(1-2\theta)^{d}I_{1/2}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right),

(E.185)

where the second line follows by (
E.181
). Since
I_{1/2}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right)=\frac{1}{2}
, it follows that
\tilde{c}(d)
is upper bounded by

\beta_{2}(d):=\frac{\pi}{2}\left(I_{\theta}\left(\frac{d+1}{2},\frac{d+1}{2}\right)+2^{d-1}(1-2\theta)^{d}\right).

(E.186)

Now
\beta_{2}(10)\approx 0.208<1-\frac{\pi}{4}
. By Proposition 4 in
[
BC21
]
, for any
x\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, the function
\alpha\mapsto I_{x}(\alpha,\alpha)
is decreasing. Hence
\beta_{2}(d)
is decreasing in
d
and thus for all
d\geqslant 10
, one has
\beta_{2}(d)\leqslant\beta_{2}(10)
.

For dimension
d=1
, it is possible to show that
c(1)=\left(\frac{5}{2}-\sqrt{2}\right)\pi-2\sqrt{2}\approx 0.583>\frac{\pi}{2}-1
. For dimension
d=2
, one can numerically estimate that
c(2)\approx 0.606>\frac{\pi}{2}-1
.

The following python code was used to carry out this estimation with an approximate error of
1.5\times 10^{-8}
. It can be used to estimate
c(d)
at other small values of
d
.

⬇

import

to

(

,

):

**

((

**2/

**2)

#

else
:

r
**

-

(

,-

,

)

#larger

cap

#

volume(intersection

of

balls)/volume(unit

#

x:

distance

between

centres

of

balls,

r1,r2:

radii,

d:

dimension

def

(

,

,

,

):

if

>=

+

:

#no

intersection

return

0

elif

<=

(

-

):

#one

ball

within

the

other

return

min
(
r1
,
r2
)**
d

else
:

c1

=

(
x
**2+
r1
**2-
